They project that the tariffs will raise inflation and slow down GDP growth, but temporarily. They projected to meet their 2% inflation target by 2026 instead of 2025 due to the tariffs. 

However, they also mentioned that the labor market remains in an equilibrium. There aren’t many hirings currently, but there’s also not many firings. This is concerning because it means that any surge in unemployment can cause the labor market to weaken. And we already know a lot of federal jobs have been cut. 

With inflation raising prices, business may have to cut their staff to save on costs. So keep an eye on the job market!

> A Cash to Debt Ratio greater than one means that the company’s operating cash flow is the same or more than their total debt. This signifies strong financial performance.

• Debt to EBITDA Ratio: 0.53 🟢
> A Debt to EBITDA Ratio less than or equal to three means that the company is able to pay back its debt in three or less years if net debt and EBITDA are held constant. This signifies the company is not over-leveraged.

### Post #134: 2025-04-03 14:53 — `$SPY`

**$SPY Market Analysis**

Quickly identify market sentiment:

Short-Term = Bearish 🔴
Long-Term = Neutral 🟡

• Sell Signal on 3/28 🔴
• HA Bearish Trend 🔴
• Below AVWAP-Q 🔴
• Below the 20 SMA🔴

The markets did not like the tariff announcement at all. In my opinion, the biggest risk in this situation is the fact that this could result in a global trade war. The second biggest risk is the impact this will have on the US economy. 

The economy is already in a tight spot, inflation above 2% with a solid labor market. Before tariffs, they were estimating the 2% inflation target to be hit this year. 

But now, that will be pushed back. The biggest concern here is the labor market. During the last FOMC, Powell said there was little hiring and little firing - keeping the labor market in equilibrium. Well, what if companies need to layoff people to reduce their expenses due to these tariffs? Now you’ll get little hiring and more firing - a rising unemployment rate. 

This could lead to a recession, which means we could see a market drop of 20-40%. 

However, the tariffs won’t go into effect for a couple of months - so the true impacts will not be known yet. I still think the most likely scenario is a 15%-20% drop from the ATH - as of now.

Downside Target: **$521.50** (15% Correction from ATH)

### Post #143: 2025-04-05 17:16 `[Dd]`

**Here’s a Study of all the times the S&P500 closed 8% or more in 1 week since 1950**

The results show the % return 1 Week, 1 Month, 3 Months, 6 Months, and 1 Year after the close of that weekly candle

Comment your thoughts below!

•The first row (yellow) includes all times

### Post #146: 2025-04-06 19:58

**How to Determine Long-Term Trend Changes**

Here is how I’ve learned to spot when a bear market and bull market has started on the daily chart of the S&P500:

1) When the 50 SMA cross below the 150 SMA and they both are sloping downwards - that signals a confirmed bear market (or longer term downtrend). Bear markets have an average drawdown of 35% and takes 282 days to bottom. See May 2022

2) When the 50 SMA crosses above the 150 SMA and they both slope up - that signals the start of a new bull market. See Feb 2023.

I’ve attached a couple of examples of each (50 SMA = blue, 150 SMA = green, both slopes need to be 10 degrees or more) 


*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/J5M/how-to-determine-long-term-tre*

### Post #152: 2025-04-09 15:05 — `$SPX, $SPY`

**Market Bottom Indicators That Work 📈**

I’ve found 3 market bottom indicators over the years that I have found to be reliable during several corrections and bear markets throughout history. And all 3 can be found on TradingView. 

These indicators may not always trigger the ultimate market bottom - but they do trigger on *A* market bottom that is followed by a rally. 

Keep in mind that no indicator is perfect - including these. Sometimes, it can take a couple of weeks before the signal completes and a bottom forms - so don’t go all in!

Also, these only work on then S&P500 chart and are not reliable for individual stocks! 

—//—
**Indicator 1: Williams %R (W%R) Confluence on the SP500 $SPX weekly chart**

Plot 2 W%R indicators - one with a 52 period and one with a 13 period. When both of these drop below -80 on the weekly chart then that signals a market bottom 

This was triggered this week ✅

**Indicator 2: Money Flow Index (MFI) on the $SPY daily chart**

This one is simple and works very well on $SPY specifically. When MFI drops below 25 - that signals a market bottom. 

This was triggered this week ✅

**Indicator 3: Normalized ATR with W%R on the SP500 $SPX weekly chart**

The ATR length is 13 and the W%R Length is also 13. When the NATR goes above 80 while the W%R goes below -80, then that signals a market bottom. This indicator specially tends to mark the ultimate bottom! 

This hasn’t been triggered yet…but it’s very close 👀 
—//—

I posted charts showing examples of all 3 indicators in this post so you can see how it works. I use these indicators for investing purposes and will buy long-term shares and LEAPs when these trigger. 

I’m sharing this bc I love the AfterHour community and want to see everyone succeed! Hope this helps you!


*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Kv2/market-bottom-indicators-that-*

**Here’s my theory on the Tariff Situation**

This is my opinion and I’m open to discussion.

The fact that Trump paused tariffs today for 90 days for most countries (except China) shows that a part of the motivation of these tariffs was to force trade negotiations. 

3 months is a long time to pause - and the inflation report tomorrow shows that it’s expected to come in lower at 2.6% from last month’s 2.8%. 

With that trajectory - inflation could come down enough for the Feds to continue cutting interest rates this June, something Trump has made clear he wants to happen.

By that time - the tariffs will have been negotiated down so that its impact on inflation and the economy will be much less than expected. 

So…we might’ve seen the market bottom folks! (Maybe)

### Post #163: 2025-04-15 15:45 — `$SPY` `[Discuss]`

**$SPY “Death” Cross**

I’ve been seeing lots of talk about the death cross (50 SMA crossing below the 200 SMA) on the $SPY Daily Chart.  

Since 1998, that death cross has happened 11 times. Well, only 3 of those times did the market continue making lower lows in a bear market/recession. That was 2000, 2008, and 2022. 

The other 8 times - that death cross actually marked the bottom (or near the bottom) of the market drop. That was 1998, 2004, 2006, 2011, 2015, 2016, 2018, and 2020.

Anything could happen, especially with the current macroeconomic backdrop, but would you take a bet with only 3/11 odds? 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/La2/dollarspy-death-cross*

---

### Post #164: 2025-04-15 22:07 — `$SPY` `[Dd]`

**$SPY “Death” Cross - Extended**

Earlier today I made a post about the dreaded Death Cross on the $SPY Daily Chart (when the 50 SMA crosses below the 200 SMA). 

I explained that when looking at the times it happened from 1998 - you see that there was a total of 11 death crosses and only 3 amounted to seeing more pain (lower lows/recession). 

Well, I extended this study back to 1960 and want to share the results:

There were a total of 28 death crosses (not including 2025) and 11 of them resulted in more pain. That’s a 39% hit rate. 

These were the years that resulted in more pain: 2022, 2008, 2000, 1984, 1981, 1977, 1973, 1971, 1969 (June), 1966, and 1962. 

Like I mentioned in my earlier post, anything can happen in the markets - but the odds of more pain is 39%. Would you bet on that?  

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Ldp/dollarspy-death-cross-extended*

### Post #166: 2025-04-16 16:11 — `$SPY` `[Dd]`

**Using the $VIX to Determine Market Bottoms**

This is a study that records the times the $VIX rose above 45 and fell below 30 after that rise and how that correlates to S&P500 ($SPY) market bottoms. The timeframe for this study is from the inception of the $VIX in 1998 to present time 2025.

__Important Notes:__
•  The "VIX High" column shows the highest $VIX reading of that week
•  The " Days < 30" column shows the number of days it took for $VIX to drop below 30
•  The results show the average number of days for $VIX to drop below 30, the % chance it signals a market bottom, and the market return 1 Month, 3 Months, 6 Months, and 1 Year from the close of that VIX high 
•  The rows in blue highlights the times $VIX rose above 60

__The Results:__
• This correlation has a 78% success rate of calling a market bottom when comparing all events, and a 67% success rate when comparing events where $VIX rose above 60 (excluding 2025)
• All returns show an average positive return 

Based off the results - whether the market bottoms or not, this signal provides a great investment opportunity with an average return of 21.5% after one year!

**Month of May Seasonality**

Seasonality is the recurring patterns of stock prices that tend to occur at specific times of the year. 

For example, it’s well known that September and October tend to be very bearish months - that’s an example of seasonality. 

This isn’t to be blindly followed, but this can help you prepare your expectations for what the market could like like throughout the year. 

Based on this, It looks like we could expect a pullback in mid to late May. If you’re looking to add to your investments, don’t chase and stay patient. We’ll get an opportunity soon. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Nlw/month-of-may-seasonality*

How do we know if this breakout on $SPY is legit and the market bottom is confirmed?

We need to see the breakout of the previous swing high hold for 5 consecutive trading days -** that level on $SPY is $576**

### Post #218: 2025-05-18 17:27 — `$SPY` `[Dd]`

**What to expect next week with the May 16, 2025 Moody's US Credit Downgrade news**

I believe we will see a pullback on Monday that might be sustained throughout the week before finding support at one of the following 3 levels on $SPY:
•  Support 1 - $576: Swing High breakout and confluence of the 100 SMA & 200 SMA (-3% move)
•  Support 2 - $571: Gap Fill (-4% move)
•  Support 3 - $554: 50 SMA (-6.5% move)

This happened 2 other times before, with downgrades from S&P and Fitch (both large credit rating agencies):

[ *Note:* Each of these past events had different economic backdrops that played a part in how the market reacted. While the market will most likely pullback on this news next week, the depth of that pullback may not be the same.]

__August 5, 2011 - S&P Downgrade__ (see chart)
•  The news was released after market close on Friday, Aug 5, 2011 (similar to today)
•  The market gapped down and dropped -6.51% on Monday

__August 2, 2011 - Fitch Downgrade__ (see chart)
•  The news was released after market close on Tuesday, Aug 2, 2011 
•  The market gapped down and dropped -1.39% on Wednesday
•  The market continued to sell-off for a total of -5% before bottoming in the short-term

__What I'll be doing:__
•  I will adding to my long-term investment portfolio at each of those levels, specifically AVGO, ODFL, CRM, AAPL, and NVDA.
•  I will also be looking to sell OTM Cash-Secured Puts (CSPs) on strong stocks that I would invest in (NVDA, AMZN, GOOGL, etc)

I also cover this is in my weekly market analysis videos on YouTube https://youtu.be/nwJUxY8cNKc

Hope this helps formulate your investing and trading plan, best of luck!

### Post #248: 2025-06-02 17:14 `[Dd]`

**Investment Opportunity: $CRM (Salesforce)**

With the market near the ATH, a lot of stocks that were undervalued are now overvalued (AVGO, MSFT, META, etc). BUT there are still opportunities out there. One of those opportunities is $CRM. Here is why:

**Financial Performance:**
> Free Cash Flow has been increasing at a 42% YoY rate the last 3 years while stock price hasn't really gone anywhere in that time
> EPS without NRI (EPS excluding non-recurring or irregular items) has also been increasing at a 39.5% YoY rate for the last 3 years
> They paid down their debt since the last quarter, making their balance sheet stronger
> They will not be affected by tariffs or trade wars as much since they are a software based company 

**Valuation:**
> With an estimated valuation of $282 per share, they are still undervalued
> Cash to Debt Ratio > 1 (operating cash flow is the same or more than their total debt)
> Debt to EBITDA Ratio < 3 (able to pay back its debt in three or less years if net debt and EBITDA are held constant - meaning they are not over-leveraged)
> Interest Coverage Ratio > 3 (can pay its interest payment on its debt 3 or more times with its operating profit. This signifies the company is poised to pay its debt as it grows)
> PEGI Ratio < 1 (The PEGI Ratio compares a company's Forward P/E to its Projected Growth relative to its industry. A PEGI Ratio < 1 suggests the stock is undervalued)

$CRM is also listed in the Dow Jones Index with a 3.86% weighting, which is a big plus.

If you're looking to add an investment in your portfolio for the next 3-5 years, this is one I would consider personally. 


*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/PhB/investment-opportunity-dollarc*

### Post #255: 2025-06-04 17:26 — `$UBER` `[Chart]`

**Opened $UBER Cash-Secured Put Trade**

Opened: 1x UBER - CSP - $82 @ 6/6 - $0.34 credit each - $34 credit

I’m basically setting a limit order at $82 that expires this Friday. I have the intention to get assigned. 

My thesis is that $UBER has broken out of its Accumulation Structure and is backtesting its breakout - which is the best point to take a trade to the long side. 

For those not familiar with the Wyckoff Methodology (highly recommend), stocks will enter consolidation phases which are either accumulation or distribution phases (where institutions are either building a position or reducing their position). The best entry for a trade is the breakout and test of the range, where $UBER is at now. 

If I don’t get assigned Friday, I may either repeat it for next Friday or just take the shares long manually. 


*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Pvd/opened-dollaruber-cash-secured*

### Post #256: 2025-06-04 17:38 — `$UBER` `[Dd]`

**$UBER Trade Idea - Breakout of Accumulation Structure**

Uber has been in a consolidation phase that looks to be confirmed as an accumulation phase. For those not familiar with the Wyckoff Methodolgy, an accumulation phase is a consolidation range where instituions are buying shares of the stock in preparation to mark prices higher. 

The best entry for this trading method is the breakout & test of the range, which is where $UBER is at currently. Also, UBER is currenly undervalued based on my valuation method and has an intrinsic value of $100/share.

If you want to take this trade idea and go long $UBER, the best point would be at $82. Otherwise the current price is fine as well. Stop Level would be a close below $80 on the daily chart.

### Post #258: 2025-06-05 02:37 — `$BTC` `[Dd]`

**Bitcoin $BTC Golden Cross Study**

I saw @average_advisor post earlier today about Bitcoin’s recent Golden Cross (50 SMA crossing above the 200 SMA) and decided to do a quick study on it to see what the results were if you bought $BTC when the cross happened. 

The study was done from Bitcoin’s inception in 2014 until present day. The Golden Cross happened a total of 10 times, with 6 of them having a sustained bull run for a decent 60% success rate. 

The average length of time that the Golden Cross lasts is 4 months (excluding the insane bull run from October 2015 to April 2018 where it gained 2,000%). 

The average % gain from Golden Cross to Death across (the reverse) is about 44.78% (again excluding the 2015 run). 

The table below summarizes all of my findings and shows what the returns were in 1 month, 3M, 6M, and 1Y from the Golden Cross. N/A means that a Death Cross happened in that timeframe. 

Hope you found this interesting @average_advisor ! 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Px8/bitcoin-dollarbtc-golden-cross*

• Fair Values are calculated using a combination of several methods including a Discounted Cash Flow model with margins of safety based on the underlying company’s business and stock performance.

__Fundamentals Rating: __**3/3**
• Cash to Debt Ratio: 1.55 🟢
> A Cash to Debt Ratio greater than one means that the company’s operating cash flow is the same or more than their total debt. This signifies strong financial performance.

• Debt to EBITDA Ratio: 0.66 🟢
> A Debt to EBITDA Ratio less than or equal to three means that the company is able to pay back its debt in three or less years if net debt and EBITDA are held constant. This signifies the company is not over-leveraged.

• Interest Coverage Ratio: 40.9 🟢
> An interest coverage ratio greater than or equal to three means that the company can pay its interest payment on its debt 3 or more times with its operating profit. This signifies the company is poised to pay its debt as it grows.

__PEGI Ratio:__ **0.57** 🔥 
• The PEGI Ratio compares a company's Forward P/E to its Projected Growth relative to its industry. A PEGI Ratio < 1 suggests the stock is undervalued.

### Post #294: 2025-06-28 16:18 — `$PLTR, $APP, $AMZN, $GOOGL` `[Dd]`

**Why some stocks surged while others dropped 10 min before market close on Friday**

The Last Friday of June is the rebalancing of the Russell indexes. Basically, they buy and sell stocks to maintain accurate weighting of each holding in their index. 

$PLTR and $APP  sold off, for example, bc their market cap has grown so much that they have to trim the holding to maintain a balanced weighting. 

$AMZN and $GOOGL surged bc they needed to increase their weighting. 

They do this every year, keep it on your calendars!


*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/RGK/why-some-stocks-surged-while-o*

### Post #297: 2025-06-29 20:35 — `$SPX, $SPY` `[Dd]`

**What is the next target for $SPY?**

Using the Trend-Based Fibonacci Extension Tool on the $SPX or $SPY Weekly chart, you can project where the next leg of the trend can go with an ultimate price target at the 1.618 Fib level. That level is $650 on $SPY (or $6,500 on $SPX). 

In my experience with this tool, I've found price to reach the 1.618 often enough to consider it valid for analysis. And market tops before a correction (10-15%) typically happen around that 1.618 level.

This does NOT mean we will just rocket up to that level. We will probably see a pullback or two (typical during new market ATH) before ultimately hitting that level. 

What this does tell me, is that I can expect the next larger pullback (10-15%) to occur somewhere around the $650 level on $SPY. No guarantees, but this is something I'll keep an eye on.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/RKl/what-is-the-next-target-for-do*

### Post #312: 2025-07-06 21:03 — `$SPY` `[Dd]`

**$SPY Tends to Dip after a new ATH**

Looking back at the $SPY weekly charts since 2008, I’m seeing that the market tends to pullback between 5-10% about 1-6 weeks after making a new ATH following  a crash. This is then typically followed by a sustained move higher in the market. 

• In 2013, we made a new ATH following the 2008-09 Bear Market. The market pulled back -6% about 3 weeks later

• In 2016, we made a new ATH following the 2015-16 crashes. The market pulled back -5% about 6 weeks later 

• In 2018, we made a new ATH following the 1st Trump Trade War with China. The market ended up crashing 20% due to the FOMC raising interest rates about 4 weeks after the new ATH.

• In 2019, we made a new ATH following the interest rate hike crash. The market pulled back -6% about 4 weeks later. 

• In 2020, we made a new ATH following the COVID Crash. The market pulled back -10% just 1 week later. 

• In 2024, we made a new ATH following the 2022 bear market. The market pulled back -5% about 6 weeks later. 

As long as a major economic event doesn’t occur (raising interest rates, trade war, tariffs, etc) - I think we’ll be able to profit by going long on that pullback. 

Nothing is guaranteed, so manage your risk! 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Sjq/dollarspy-tends-to-dip-after-a*

### Post #329: 2025-07-15 15:13 — `$NVDA, $META`

**For those who are feeling $NVDA FOMO**

$NVDA will eventually pullback, so be ready for that. It’s had an incredible rally since the April lows and is definitely not sustainable. But keep in mind you’ll most likely be buying back in at a higher price than you sold. 

If you regret selling your shares, take this time to reflect on why you decided to sell your shares instead of holding. Investing is a long-term game (5 years +) and requires a mindset of looking past P/L. 

If your reasons include: 
• it’s gone up too much and must come down 
• I’m tired of holding it while it went nowhere for a year
• I’ll sell at the top and buy it back when it dips

Then you’re not in the investor mindset. 

The only reasons to sell an investment is if you see a breakdown in the fundamentals of the company (lost huge market share, obsolete products, financial fraud, etc.) 

I’ve made that same mistake many times (like with $META), but that’s ok because that’s how you learn. The market will always present opportunities, the key is to learn from your mistakes and grow! 💪

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Tg4/for-those-who-are-feeling-doll*

### Post #333: 2025-07-16 14:47 — `$SPX` `[Dd]`

**The Gold/Silver Ratio and How it Signals a Sustained Market Bull Run**

I came across a video that analyzed the relationship between Gold and Silver on the charts (this can be done on TradingView). 

The concept is that Silver is a more speculative investment vehicle for investors in the precious metals space. That means when the markets are “risk on” they will rotate from Gold to Silver. When that happens, the Gold/Silver Ratio would start declining. An aggressive rotation into Silver would cause this chart to drop bigly (>14% in a few weeks) - which theoretically would mean investors are anticipating a sustained move higher in the markets. 

When plotting the Rate of Change (ROC) indicator on the Gold/Silver chart on the Weekly timeframe (which simply measures the % move over a set number of weeks), it can signal a large bullish move in the markets in the medium-term. 

I wanted to test this, so I went back to 1995 and measured how the market ($SPX) performed every time the Gold/Silver Ratio plotted a ROC of -14% or more. 

The results were impressive: 

• There were a total of 16 triggers - 13 of them were successful! That’s a win rate of 81%
• The avg 1 Month Return is 2.38%
• The avg 3 Month Return is 6.23%
• The avg 6 Month Return is 10.42%
• The avg 1 Year Return is 15.31% 

(All returns were measured on the S&P500 Index $SPX)

The good news is we just had a trigger occur last week 👀 - so a 10% move from now until the end of the year is in the cards 🔥


*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Tqk/the-goldsilver-ratio-and-how-i*

### Post #344: 2025-07-20 23:31

**UPDATE: New Swing Trading Signals ⚠️**

As some of you may know, I've been working on my very own trading signals for quite some time now. I've probably spent over 2 years putting together a system to reliably take long swing trades, with the past 6 months being a live testing phase for these trades. 

The idea is that I will post an alert to take a swing trade around 30 min before market close. The trade is designed to be held for 24 hours and closed on the next trading day at the same time (~30 min before market close). This strategy has been shown to boast a solid win rate with every month being profitable in the past 6 months during my live testing.

I would not post a trade alert that I myself wouldn't use. __So starting August 1__, I will be posting these alerts and taking them myself publicly (both in my Discord and here on AH). You do not have to follow these signals as I cannot guarantee results just yet, but I wanted to establish a track record that is visible to the public (not just on my spreadsheets). These will be long trades only.

### Post #355: 2025-07-25 18:23 — `$NVDA, $UBER, $NVO, $HIMS`

**Smaller accounts (<$10k) can also make money with my trading method**

I use about $100k for my trading, which mostly consists of option selling with some swing trading too. I’m well aware that is not the norm, and I understand that some traders might feel like it’s not worth it unless they have more capital (that’s how I felt when I started). 

My personal style is more conservative - I tend execute my trades with a market pullback being accounted for. This makes it easier for new traders to follow and build confidence. It also helps with trading psychology - since you know you’re being assigned at a good price if the market does pullback. 

With that in mind, I’ve been making sure I also trade tickers with good fundamentals that are less than $100/share. That means you don’t have to put up more than $10k collateral to sell an option. 

**Thats because my goal on here is to help traders of all sizes become profitable.**

I went back to March 2025 and only looked at the trades I made with stocks with that criteria. 

Those names are (or were in the case of $NVDA):
• $HIMS
• $UBER
• $NVO
• $NVDA

**My performance from March 2025 - July 2025 with those trades is a 95% Win Rate with a total return of $1,027 (or 10.3%).**

Not too shabby, if I do say so myself! 

I post all my trades on my free Discord, so feel free to comfirm this yourself. Thanks for reading!

### Post #369: 2025-07-30 16:21 — `$UNH` `[Chart]`

**$UNH Technical Analysis | Possible $225 Target**

$UNH hardly ever sells off and breaks the 50 SMA on the Monthly Timeframe. The last time this happened was in the 2009 Financial Crisis, where it found support at the 200 SMA on the Monthly Timeframe. It formed a bottom there over the course of ~6 months before making a recovery. $UNH broke the 150 SMA, and will likely be heading to the 200 SMA which is at ~$225. 

It took $UNH just under 2 years to break back above all major SMA's on the monthly.

$UNH is still great on the fundamental side, with a div yield of over 3% at this price point. The reputation of the healthcare industry is damaged from their business practices over the last decade, so there will be tailwinds as the stock recovers.

### Post #374: 2025-07-31 21:20

**Swing Trading Signals: Full Guide 📖**

Below is a full guide on how I trade my Swing Signals ⬇️

**Signal Guide**
• This is a long-only swing trading system, built for simplicity and ease of execution.

• Trade Alerts are Posted 30 minutes before market close (12:30pm PST) Monday–Thursday in my Discord and here on AfterHour 

• Hold Time: 
Most trades are closed the next day 30 minutes before market close (12:30pm PST). Some may close early morning (7:00am PST) or before major events (e.g., FOMC) — details will always be included.

•  Performance Updates: 
I will post exit updates and results for every trade. I’ll be utilizing $1,000 capital for each trade alert using options (or $10,000 if using shares). 

—//—
I trade my system using Shares, ITM Calls, or Call Debit Spreads:

1. Shares
• Choose how much I’m willing to **risk** (not total capital) to determine how many shares to buy.
•  Using the stat of 0.7% average loss per trade:
Formula: Risk ÷ 0.007 = Total capital allocated per trade
• Example: 
- $100 risk → $100 / 0.007 = ~$14,300 in shares
- Stock A is $50 per share → $14,300 / $50 = 286 Shares

2. In-the-Money (ITM) Calls
• Buy an ITM Call with 0.7 Delta and 21–25 DTE
• Simulates 10x exposure vs. shares.
• Example: 
- I'm willing to risk $100 per trade. That means I would need $14,300 in shares. 
- However if I use ITM calls, then I will only need $1,430 worth to simulate the same returns as those shares.

3. Call Debit Spreads (CDS)
•  Buy an At-The-Money (ATM) Call with 0.5 Delta *AND* Sell an Out-The-Money (OTM) Call with 0.2 Delta with 30–45 days DTE
• Cost-effective method, great for high-priced tickers (e.g., MSFT)
• Also simulates 10x exposure vs. shares

—//—

I will take every trade I post — I only share signals I believe in. If performance drops, I’ll pause and adjust the system.

Let’s get it 🚀

https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Vr2/swing-trading-signals-going-li is

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Vxo/swing-trading-signals-full-gui*

### Post #409: 2025-08-09 21:33

**Technical Indicators that Work: Heikin-Ashi (HA) Candles | Part 1**

Some of you requested I make some posts about technical analysis, so I wanted to start a series called “Technical Indicators that Work.” Title is self-explanatory lol.

This one is about one of my favorites to analyze trends and reduce noise surrounding whether a trend will continue or not - HA Candles (Part 1) ⬇️ 

__Key Points__
•    HA candles smooth out price action and can be used to identify trends.
•    There are three types of HA candles: bullish (flat-bottom), bearish (flat-top), and indecision (doji).
•    When HA candles form a particular pattern, they can be used to identify trend reversals and trend continuations. 
•    HA candles help both investors and traders by identifying more efficient entries into the market.

**What are HA Candles?**
Heikin-Ashi (HA), which translates to "average bar" in Japanese, is a type of candlestick used to smooth out price action and make it easier to identify trends. Unlike traditional candlesticks, which are plotted using the open, high, low, and close prices of each period, HA candles are calculated using a modified formula that incorporates data from the previous candle.

The HAcandle is calculated using the following formulas:
•    Close Price: (Open + High + Low + Close) / 4
•    Open Price: (Previous Open + Previous Close) / 2
•    High Price: Maximum of (High, HA Open, HA Close)
•    Low Price: Minimum of (Low, HA Open, HA Close)

These formulas result in candles that are smoother and easier to interpret, filtering out much of the market noise and highlighting the underlying trend.

**How to Read HA Candles**
Heikin-Ashi candles can be read similarly to traditional candlesticks but with a focus on the overall trend rather than individual price movements. Here’s how to interpret them:
1.    Bullish Candles: Also referred to ask Flat-Bottom Candles. Typically have no lower wicks and indicate a strong upward trend. 
2.    Bearish Candles: Also referred to as Flat-Top candles. Typically have no upper wicks and indicate a strong downward trend. 
3.    Indecision Candles: Also referred to as Doji Candles. Small bodies with wicks on both sides suggest a potential reversal or consolidation phase. 

Those are the 3 ways that HA candles are interpreted for the purposes of our trading.w

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Wrv/technical-indicators-that-work*

### Post #410: 2025-08-09 23:44

**Technical Indicators that Work: Heikin-Ashi (HA) Candles | Part 2**

Link to Part 1 at the end of this post! 

**Bullish Trend Reversal**
A bullish trend reversal pattern can be identified by the following sequence:
1.    An existing downtrend formed by a series of Bearish Candles.
2.    The appearance of a Doji Candle following the last Bearish Candle in the existing downtrend. It doesn’t matter if the Doji Candle is green or red.
3.    The appearance of a Bullish Candle following the Doji Candle or a series of Doji Candles.
4.    The appearance of a second consecutive Bullish Candle.

This indicates that the existing downtrend may reverse into an uptrend.

**Technical Indicators that Work: Heikin-Ashi (HA) Candles | Part 3**

Part 2 can be found in the link below 

**Bearish Trend Reversal**
A bearish trend reversal pattern is essentially the inverse of the bullish trend reversal pattern, as seen below:
1.    An existing uptrend formed by a series of Bullish Candles.
2.    The appearance of a Doji Candle following the last Bullish Candle in the existing uptrend. It doesn’t matter if the Doji Candle is green or red.
3.    The appearance of a Bearish Candle following the Doji Candle or a series of Doji Candles.
4.    The appearance of a second consecutive Bearish Candle.

This indicates that the existing uptrend may reverse into a downtrend.

### Post #413: 2025-08-10 18:25

**Technical Indicators that Work: Heikin-Ashi (HA) Candles | Part 4 - Continuation Patterns**

Link for Part 3 at the end of the post! (Parts 2 and 3 cover Trend Reversal Patterns using HA Candles)

**Bullish Continuation**
A bullish continuation pattern can be identified by the following sequence:
1.    An existing uptrend formed by a series of Bullish Candles.
2.    The appearance of a consolidation period shown by one or more Doji Candles following the last Bullish Candle in the existing uptrend. It doesn’t matter if the Doji Candle(s) are green or red.
3.    The appearance of a Bullish Candle with a second consecutive Bullish Candle.
> Sometimes, a single Bullish or Bearish Candle can appear in between multiple Doji Candles. This is why it’s important to wait for the second consecutive Bullish or Bearish Candle for the highest odds of success in identifying what the trend will be.

This indicates that the consolidation period may be over and the current trend will continue.


**Bearish Continuation**
A bearish continuation pattern is also essentially the inverse of the bullish continuation pattern, as seen below:
1.    An existing downtrend formed by a series of Bearish Candles.
2.    The appearance of a consolidation period shown by one or more Doji Candles following the last Bearsh Candle in the existing downtrend. It doesn’t matter if the Doji Candle(s) are green or red.
3.    The appearance of a Bearishish Candle with a second consecutive Bearish Candle.
> Sometimes, a single Bullish or Bearish Candle can appear in between multiple Doji Candles. This is why it’s important to wait for the second consecutive Bullish or Bearish Candle for the highest odds of success in identifying what the trend will be.

This indicates that the consolidation period may be over and the current trend will continue.

### Post #421: 2025-08-12 15:59 — `$UBER, $AAPL`

**Technical Indicators that Work: Heikin-Ashi (HA) Candles | Part 5 - Examples and Conclusion**

Link to Part 4 at the bottom of this post.
—//—

• Example 1: $UBER Long Trade - April 10, 2025
In the first image, a long trade was entered at $71 based on the HA Bullish Reversal plot on the daily chart. Throughout the hold, you see periods with mixed overlapping candles and small dips.  

But you don’t get shaken out, thanks to HA Candles. You hold all the way until $88 where a HA Bearish Reversal pattern shows up.

• Example 2: $AAPL Short Trade - December 30, 2024
In the second image, a short trade was initiated at $252 based on the HA Bearish Reversal plot. 

Just like example 1, you don’t get shaken out on any counter-trend bounces that appear until you see the HA Bullish Reversal pattern and exit at $230. 

**Conclusion**
HA candles are a valuable tool for traders, providing a clearer view of the market’s underlying trend by smoothing out price action. By understanding how they are plotted and learning to read their patterns, traders can better identify trends, potential reversals, and continuation patterns. While HA candles can simplify trading decisions, it’s important to use them in conjunction with other technical analysis tools and risk management strategies to make well-informed trading decisions. It’s also important to note that no technical indicator can guarantee a 100% success rate. Our objective is not to always be right, but to increase our probability of success as much as possible.

Thanks for reading! Now use this knowledge to squeeze more juice out of your winning trades 💪

### Post #446: 2025-08-18 17:22 — `$NVDA`

**Technical Indicators that Work: Anchored VWAP | Part 1**

Anchored Volume Weighted Average Price (aka AVWAP) is one of my favorite technical indicators. Price will almost always react to this indicator, as long as you anchor it to the right spot. That's where this guide comes in.

__Key Points:__
• AVWAP provides reliable support and resistance levels in the short and long-term, based on the anchor period.
• AVWAP helps identify bullish or bearish structure for both trading and investing.
• The 3 anchor points I use are: __Earnings__ (individual stocks), __Quarter__ (market indices), and __Year__ (both stocks and indices).

**What is AVWAP?**
AVWAP calculates the average price of a stock weighted by volume, starting from a specific point in time (the anchor) such as earnings, a breakout, or market event. It helps traders and investors see the true average price buyers and sellers have paid since that chosen event.

Take a look at this $NVDA  chart from 2024. The yellow line plotted on the chart is **AVWAP-Y** (VWAP anchored to the beginning of the year). NVDA touched that level only twice in 2024, but both times ended up being excellent entry points!

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Xgg/technical-indicators-that-work*

### Post #451: 2025-08-19 21:37

**Technical Indicators that Work: Anchored VWAP | Part 2**

Link to Part 1:
https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Xgg/technical-indicators-that-work

**How to Analyze Charts with AVWAP?**
Simply put:
•  If price is trading above AVWAP, then buyers are in control and the stock is in a bullish short-term structure. 
•  If price is trading below AVWAP, then sellers are in control and the stock is in a bearish short-term structure. 

You can also use them as support levels for longer term swing trades or investments. Just put an alert on that line in TradingView and you'll be alerted when a stock you've been watching hits it.

**My Favorite Anchor Points**
•  __Earnings__:  (AVWAP-E) Used for individual stocks - This acts as a very strong support or resistance level for shorter term trading.
•  __Year__: (AVWAP-Y) Used for both individual stocks and the market indices -This acts as a very strong support or resistance level for longer term trading/investing.
•  __Quarter__: (AVWAP-Q) Used only for the market indices (SPY, QQQ, etc) - This helps with determining whether the market as whole is in a shorter term bullish or bearish structure.

TradingView has free AVWAP indicators where you can have it automatically set to anchor at each of those points for you.

**Example 1**
__Bullish Structure Example with AVWAP-E:__
Below is the daily chart of JNJ with the AVWAP-E indicator, which is anchored to JNJ's latest Earnings Report on July 16, 2025. Notice how price is trading above AVWAP-E starting on July 18, 2025 - that signals a bullish structure. That means that we expect AVWAP-E to act as support and for price to bounce off and continue moving higher. And that's exactly what we've been seeing.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Xox/technical-indicators-that-work*

Using AVWAP-Q on $SPY  is foundational for my market analysis - as most stocks will follow what SPY does. If SPY is in a bearish structure, most stocks will be bearish - and vice versa for the bullish case.

### Post #467: 2025-08-25 23:12 — `$AAPL`

**How to Buy Calls the Right Way 📈**

I remember the first time I wanted to buy a call option on a stock I was bullish on. I opened that options chain and made this face 😵‍💫 So many strikes and expirations to choose from, which is the best combination?

To answer this, I’ll briefly go over what a call option contract is. A call option is a contract that gives the buyer the right (not the obligation) to purchase a stock at a predetermined price within a specific period.

Let’s say you’re bullish on $AAPL which is currently at $227. You buy a $225 call option that expires next Friday on 9/5 for $1,000. You’re basically saying that you believe Apple will be at or more than $225 by next Friday. I’d say that’s pretty likely as long as the market continues to move higher. If you’re right and $AAPL moves to $230, the value of your option contract will be higher (maybe $2,000) since now you can instantly make $5 profit per share if you execute the contract (AKA In-The-Money). Each contract is 100 shares. 

THIS IS WHY OUT OF THE MONEY CALLS ARE NOT SMART. Sure they’re cheap, but for a reason - Market Makers deem it as unlikely. Would you execute a $250 call on Apple when it’s trading at $235? Why would you, you’d instantly lose $15 a share if you execute. 

let’s use a similar example like the one above. 

Let’s say you instead buy a $245 call option on $AAPL for 9/5 and it costs you only $250. You’re basically saying that for you to BREAK EVEN on the shares, Apple will need to trade at $245 by next Friday. You’re not putting the odds in your favor, and your call option contract will de-value everyday Apple trades below $245 (AKA Time Decay).

Here’s how to construct your call options to avoid time decay and maximize your gains:

**In-The-Money (ITM) Call:**
• Buy an ITM call that has at least 0.7 Delta 
• Make sure expiration is at least 21 days away
• This simulates 10x exposure vs shares and minimizes time decay as you hold

**Call Debit Spread**
• Buy an At-The-Money (ATM) call with 0.5 Delta AND Sell an Out-The-Money (OTM) call with 0.2 Delta
• Open the above legs at the same time 
• Make sure expiration is 30-45 days away
• Also simulates 10x exposure vs shares at a fraction of the cost of an ITM Call



*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/XXo/how-to-buy-calls-the-right-way*

### Post #480: 2025-08-28 23:19

**How I Select Strikes for Cash-Secured Puts (CSPs)**

First rule: don’t sell puts on garbage stocks. Fundamentals & technicals must check out. You should be *willing to own* shares if assigned.

Selecting strikes is all about finding strong support levels that the stock will have a high chance of reacting to. I use: 
• 100 / 150 / 200 SMAs
• Anchored VWAP (Anchored to Earnings and/or the Beginning of the Year)

Look at past price action and see: which SMA does the stock respect most? That’s usually your best candidate. My favorite setups are when support levels line up. 100 SMA + AVWAP-Y at the same price = high-probability support.

I also like to set strikes at a stock’s fair value, which I determine from my valuation models. I post those in my Discord (link in bio). This helps me take assignment knowing that I’m buying the stock at a fair price for a longer term hold. 

Remember, pick strikes where you want assignment. I only roll/close early if support clearly breaks down. If you just want premium & lower assignment risk, then pick strikes that are below at least 2 support levels (e.g. 50 SMA + AVWAP-E). This will give you some buffer as the price will chop around before heading lower to your strike.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/Yg2/how-i-select-strikes-for-cash-*

We're entering two of the most bearish months of the year historically (September and October). Don't be surprised to see heightened volatility, bad news, and market pullbacks.

**Why I don’t charge for my Discord**

…Yet. 

Right now, my priority is building a strong community, earning trust, and providing value without asking for anything in return. I want people to learn, grow, and benefit risk-free.

I know my knowledge is valuable. Many of you have told me I should be charging for the insights I share. I’ve also heard that keeping it free might make it seem less credible or “not worth paying for.” And I get that.

But for the last year, I’ve been focused on helping people first and letting the results speak for themselves.

That being said, with the launch of swing trading signals and more advanced tools, there will come a time when I begin charging. And when I do, the goal is for the subscription to pay for itself.

Until then, take full advantage of what I’m sharing while it’s still free: https://discord.gg/AZFcxUKUQb

__Fair Value:__ **$216** *Grade A*
• Fair Values are calculated using a combination of several methods including a Discounted Cash Flow model with margins of safety based on the underlying company’s business and stock performance.

__Fundamentals Rating__: **2/3**
• Cash to Debt Ratio: 0.59 🔴
> A Cash to Debt Ratio greater than one means that the company’s operating cash flow is the same or more than their total debt. This signifies strong financial performance.

• Debt to EBITDA Ratio: 2.23 🟢
> A Debt to EBITDA Ratio less than or equal to three means that the company is able to pay back its debt in three or less years if net debt and EBITDA are held constant. This signifies the company is not over-leveraged.

• Interest Coverage Ratio: 5.73 🟢
> An interest coverage ratio greater than or equal to three means that the company can pay its interest payment on its debt 3 or more times with its operating profit. This signifies the company is poised to pay its debt as it grows.

__PEGI Ratio:__ **1.59** 
• The PEGI Ratio compares a company's Forward P/E to its Projected Growth relative to its industry. A PEGI Ratio < 1 suggests the stock is undervalued.

__Ranking in the S&P500:__ **#6**

__Technical Outlook:__ **Bullish**

PPI (Producer Price Index - another measure of inflation) came out this morning below epxectations. It was forecasted to come in at 0.3% increase from last month, but actually came in at -0.1% lower than last month. That is bullish news and suggests that the inflation reading tomorrow may also come in cooler than expected.

The reason why this is bullish is because the Feds will be cutting rates to prevent the continued weakening of the labor market. When interest rates are cut in an economy that is doing fine, inflation runs the risk of moving higher. If inflation comes in cooler, it makes the Feds more confident to continue cutting rates.

### Post #539: 2025-09-12 18:39 — `$HIMS` `[Chart]`

**$HIMS Daily Chart - Wyckoff Structure**

$HIMS looks to be confirming that it is trading within a consolidation range between $66 and $41. We've seen price react and hold these two levels multiple times now. 

Wyckoff Structure means that institutions are either accumulating shares in preparation for a move higher - or they are distributing shares to prepare for a move lower. There's no way to tell until we see price breakout and successfully test and hold the breakout. 

Set alerts at these levels!

### Post #544: 2025-09-14 15:57

**Allow Me to Re-Introduce Myself**

I hit 1k followers on here recently and feel like this a good time to share more about who I am and what I do. 

I work full-time as an engineer (I love numbers), am married, and really enjoy passing on knowledge. I got into the stock market as an investor in 2018 and as a trader in 2020. Like many of us, I spent countless hours speaking with others and searching the internet to try and learn the skills I needed to be a successful investor and trader. 

While I was able to learn basics, my passion allowed me to develop (and continue developing) the advanced skills needed to be successful in the markets. That came at the cost of lots of time and money - costs that I want to help others avoid.

So I made Market Victor as a grounded community for people like me: those who work full-time and want to build a secure financial future. You don't have time to day trade and you can't afford taking on big losses. I try to pass on the mindset and system that has helped me consistently generate monthly income and build a long-term portfolio - all while sleeping soundly at night. The key for me is to blend the investing and trading mindset into one. 

So thank you for finding value in what I do. If you resonate with anything I shared, then consider following me and checking out my Discord. It's currently free and I'm sure you'll find value there!

https://discord.gg/AZFcxUKUQb

### Post #555: 2025-09-18 05:46

**Quick Summary of Today’s FOMC**

Powell said this economy is challenging for policy makers. Usually inflation is below 2% when they decide to cut rates, yet here we have inflation heightened near 3%.

According to the Fed, they will now reach their 2% target by June 2028. They project inflation to be at 2.6% by June 2026.

They also project that unemployment remain below 5% through 2028 and GDP remains just below the 2% level. 

The Feds plan on cutting rates 2 more times this year, for a total cut of 0.75% in 2025. They believe the risk of employment weakling is higher than inflation rising at this point. 

What about the stock market?
• studies show that when the feds cut rates when stocks are near ATH, the market is up by a year later by 15% on average 16 OUT OF 16 Times - 100% Rate!

BUY DIPS AND STAY INVESTED 🫡

### Post #574: 2025-09-24 02:56

**The Importance of an Economic Moat**

When building your investment portfolio, one of the most important metrics to look at is the strength of the company’s economic moat.

An economic moat is the company’s sustainable competitive advantage that protects it from competitors. 

Think Microsoft - what operating system do you use at work? School? Home? Most likely Windows. If not - then most likely an Apple product (another company with a huge economic moat).

What software do you read PDFs in? Yep, Adobe. Kind of wild to think about!

Next time you buy shampoo or detergent, look at the label. You’ll probably either see Proctor & Gamble or Johnson & Johnson. 

This consideration will help you narrow down your watchlist of stocks to invest in and is considered one of the most important qualities to look for in order to have a secure portfolio for the next 5-10 years. 

It’s definitely one of the top things I look for in my investments! 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/1Fm/the-importance-of-an-economic-*

### Post #579: 2025-09-25 00:28 — `$SPY` `[Dd]`

**Sooo Where’s the Pullback?**

It’s a known phenomenon that August-September tends to be the weakest months of the year in terms of market performance (AKA seasonality). It’s usually volatile and consists of a drawdown in the markets.

I attached pictures from 2021 and 2023 that show examples of this, but look back on the $SPY charts and you’ll see many more examples. 

So far this year, we’ve had no substantial pullback! I was doing a Quick Look on the charts and saw that the last time we haven’t seen weakness or volatility in this period was 2017.

In 2017, the market was able to continue rallying throughout the end of the year and didn’t pullback until Jan 2019. And it pulled back hard (~12%).

Nobody knows what will happen this year, and there’s still time for a pullback to happen. But just wanted to share something I saw last night and what the market could look like if we don’t pullback before October 31. 


*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/1Pi/sooo-wheres-the-pullback*

### Post #584: 2025-09-26 14:47 — `$SPY`

**$SPY Daily Market Analysis**

Quickly identify market sentiment:

Short-Term = Neutral 🟡 
Long-Term = Bullish 🟢

• Bullish Cycle on 9/4 🟢
• HA Doji 🟡 
• Above AVWAP-Q 🟢
• Above the 20 SMA 🟢
> Stay cautiously long 

PCE Price Index (another measure of inflation) came in as expected at 0.2% (meaning an increase by 0.2% since last month). The markets like it when reports come in as expected, because it means their current valuations and projections are accurate.

So this week showed us that GDP was higher than expected, with inflation remaining elevated and a labor market weakening. The economy doesn't feel great to people like us (and a lot of industries are not doing well - trucking, healthcare). So why is the market up?

The market is up because corporate profits are making record highs quarter after quarter (specifically in the tech sector). Ultimately, that's what the stock market is based on. If corporate profits start to contract quarter after quarter, that's when the market would correct or enter into a bear market - which is why it is a leading indicator of recessions.

Anyways, until then - stay long!

On the topic of government shutdowns - this situation happens every year. Usually, they agree on funding at the last minute (would is Sept 30 midnight). 

In the case that the government does shutdown, let's look at a couple of historical cases where the markets were near ATH:
1) January 19, 2019 - gov shutdown for 2 days under Trump. Market didn't drop at all.
2) October 1, 2013 - gov shutdown for 16 days under Obama. Market pulled back -2.21% before ending higher at 2.2% when the shutdown ended

The shutdowns in 2018 occurred AFTER the market already dropped, so can't really look at those (although, the market rallied during the shutdown in both cases).

### Post #605: 2025-10-02 17:29 — `$SPY`

**October Seasonality $SPY**

Seasonality in the market refers to the general performance of the market on a monthly basis. This is done by aggregating data in a certain timeframe and plotting the average price movement each month. 

The one I’m sharing with you from Equity Clock aggregates 20 Years of data with the range ending 12/31/2024. 

October tends to be the last bearish month of the year. If the market pulls back in September - the market will tend to bottom and rally sometime in October (usually mid-October). 

For the first time in 7 years, the market did not pullback in September. I might make another post analyzing times in the past where the market didn’t drop in September and see what we can expect for the rest of the year. 

For now, I’m going to stay cautious until mid-October where I’ll start trading a bit more aggressively again. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/25X/october-seasonality-dollarspy*

### Post #624: 2025-10-07 15:52 — `$SPY`

**$SPY has broken AVWAP-Q | Risk of a pullback has increased.**

I want to see confirmation on the daily candle close.

White Line = AVWAP-Q. 

AVWAP-Q is the Volume Weighted Average Price that is anchored to the beginning of each quarter. This indicator is used on the $SPY index to determine short-term market structure. 

When price trades above AVWAP-Q, buyers are in control and I expect a bullish trend in the short term.

When price trades below AVWAP-Q, sellers are in control and I expect a bearish trend in the short term. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/35D/dollarspy-has-broken-avwap-q-o*

### Post #647: 2025-10-11 00:52 `[Discuss]`

**How I Handled the Market Drop Today**

If you've been following my posts, then you know that I've been staying cautious and expecting a market pullback since August. My patience has finally paid off and we are getting the pullback we've been waiting for.

Why was I expecting a pullback? Because August-October is the weakest seasonal period of the year - the market has a high chance of pulling back during this time. On top of that, we haven’t gotten any pullback since April - that’s rare. 

Because I've had a neutral short-term outlook, I've been taking bullish trades cautiously - meaning I was expecting a pullback at any time. This means that the strikes I was choosing my cash-secured puts on were conservative and only at prices where I would be happy taking assignment on a big drop. This also means that most of my open positions right now are covered calls. I even added some shares of AMZN and CPRT to my long-term portfolio after market close today.

My cautious approach has paid off because now I am positioned to take advantage of this drop. I'm not scrambling to close positions - I only closed one open CSP I had on AMZN as I didn't want to over-lever myself. I have a clear focus to look at support levels to use my cash I've been accumulating and adding to my long-term ports and a clear focus to sell cash-secured puts on stocks I want to own now that premiums are elevated.

I'm not sharing this to boast - and I hope it doesn't come off this way. I'm posting this to explain how I positioned myself for success during a red day like this. I'm sharing this so you can learn from the way I've been approaching the markets and how it's helped me not get screwed on days like this.

The markets are about survival - risk management is key!

### Post #652: 2025-10-12 18:52 — `$VEEV`

**$VEEV Swing Trade Review | Wyckoff Trading**

This post is to go over how and why I took this successful trade on $VEEV.

The Wyckoff Method is one of my favorite trading methods - and it's very beginner friendly. The basic idea is that market makers own a warehouse of stocks that they have to fill. They do this in consolidation structures that are easily spotted. For $VEEV, it has been trading between $291.68 and $271 since June 2025.

You can range trade this pattern by going long at $271 and selling at $291.68 (which I've done previously) and you can also trade the breakout. 

According to the Wyckoff Method, the key indication of a breakout is when you see the "Spring Test" AKA a False Breakout and Recovery. We saw this in early September and I decided to start building a long positon during that test around $271. As $VEEV continued to move higher, I added to my position and waited to see if we get a breakout and test at $291.68 - and we did.

I added a final batch on 9/30 when $VEEV closed the daily candle above $291.68. My plan was to hold this swing until ATH - as multi-month Wyckoff ranges like this certainly have the potential to rally strongly.

However, the market dropped on Friday due to trade war news with China. This caused $VEEV to drop back into the range - invalidating the breakout. That was my signal to close the position for a gain and wait for the next opportunity.

If you want to learn more about the Wyckoff Method - I provide a guide for free in my Discord (link in bio).

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/5aV/dollarveev-swing-trade-review-*

### Post #655: 2025-10-13 14:56 — `$SPY` `[Dd]`

**New Market Study - Historical Market Performance following a 2% or more Market Drop**

I performed a study over the last 15 years (2010 - 2025) of all the times the S&P500 Index posted a Daily Candle of -2% or more __Above the 50 SMA__ (which closely replicates what we saw on 10/10/25).

These are the results:
- There were a total of 14 instances that fit this criteria
- 12 of those 14 **resulted in more downside**
- The average % drawdown from the open of the candle to the bottom was** -6.63%**
- The average number of days it took for the market to bottom was **16 days**

According to this, __there is an 85% chance that we see more downside__ following the move on 10/10/25. That would put a price target of **$627 on $SPY**. Let's see what happens!

Remember: as an investor and trader, it's not your job to predict what the markets will do - but rather prepare for any likely scenario to happen so you can take advantage of the opportunities presented to you.

### Post #668: 2025-10-16 01:02

**Why I added $JNJ to my Investment Portfolio**

$JNJ has been on my watchlist as a healthcare company for a while. It's been in my Valuation Database for years, but has been in a long-term downtrend for a lot of that time. That downtrend was signaled by the 50 SMA crossing below the 150 SMA with both sloping down by at least 10 degrees. 

That is, until August of this year where the long-term uptrend signal was formed by the 50 SMA (Blue) crossing above the 150 SMA (Green) AND both were sloping up by at least 10 degrees.

So I knew that $JNJ is a great healthcare business, has a strong market presence, growing financials, great dividend, and offers a great defensive stock to include in my port. $JNJ was also trading around it's fair value at the time of $165.

 So I knew I was paying a good price, but I wanted to time my investment better so that I DCA up and not down. So when I saw that technical confirmation - I pulled the trigger on August 4, 2025.

Like I always do, I started with a 50% position and have been DCA-ing up since then.

If you're looking for a great healthcare/defensive dividend paying stock for your portfolio - then $JNJ is a great choice. Don't worry that it's at an ATH - what's more important is that it's still trading within 10% of it's valuation at $175. Every stock was at ATH at some point, the key for investing is knowing that the stock you pick will move well beyond that ATH in the coming years.

### Post #672: 2025-10-16 23:11 — `$SPY`

**$SPY Analysis is Extremely Important for Trading Success**

75%+ of stocks will follow what $SPY is doing bc the S&P500 Index is representative of the stock market. Therefore, if you can analyze and read $SPY properly - then you can execute your trades much better. 

For example, if you know that $SPY is in a bullish long-term technical structure - then you will look at dips as opportunities to add to your investments. 

Another example, If you know that $SPY is in a bearish short term structure (price trading below AVWAP-Q like it currently has been this whole week) - then you know to expect prices to head lower and sell cash-secured puts at support levels (like the 50 SMA).

$SPY is your guide, and it’s why I post so much TA on it. I want one of my contributions on here to provide clarity on market movements, so that you can be focused on the right side of the market and make informed decisions 🫡

### Post #706: 2025-10-29 19:27

**FOMC Key Notes 10/29/2025**

- Fed cuts rates by 25 bps as expected. Economic conditions haven't changed much from September's meeting.

- Fed decided to end Quantitative Tightening on Dec 1, 2025. This adds another bullish tailwind as the Fed will stop reducing the size of their balance sheet by selling or maturing gov bonds. This means that they see the risk of inflation to continue to lower.

- The chance of another rate cut in Dec dropped to 82%. Powell said the cut is not a forgone conclusion. According to Powell, after today's cut they are in the range of the rate cut consensus that is in the 300-400 range. Some committee members have differing views on whether to cut or pause here. The main point of differing views is __risk tolerances.__  They need to evaluate the labor market and mentioned to "wait a cycle" before cutting rates again. This was the main source of the market drop today.

- Labor supply has declined, and the Fed's tool affects the Labor __demand__ side. Meaning, rate cuts affect job creation - which is also very low. A factor of labor supply declining is a reduction in immigration. 

- Inflation from tariffs is a one-off. They expect 2-4 more tenths of inflation increasing and then a stop. That also means prices will stop going up too. As real income goes up over time, consumers will be able to afford more again.

- Not too much leverage in the banking system. Aggregate household debt levels are also acceptable, even when accounting for rising defaults in low income brackets. 

- Powell said this market is different than the 1990's since the __large__ AI companies actually have earnings. Back in 1990's, the companies were only ideas with no earnings. 

- Labor market weakens is __not__ accelerating. Or any part of the economy for that matter. They will resume rate cuts at some point.

In my opinion, we'll still get a rate cut in December.

### Post #707: 2025-10-30 01:34 — `$UBER, $AMD`

**How to use ATR (Average True Range) - Part 1**

This will be a 2 or 3 part post on how to use a foundational technical indicator in your trading - Average True Range (ATR). 

Read on below ⬇️

The ATR indicator is a technical analysis tool that measures market volatility. It's very useful for determining Profit Target, Stop Loss, and even Strike Selection for Option Selling. I'll go over all that for you in this guide.

__How is ATR Calculated?__
ATR calculates the average range of price movements over a specific number of periods, typically 14. It takes the greatest value of the following and averages them together:
- The current high minus the current low
- The absolute value of the current high minus the previous close
- The absolute value of the current low minus the previous close

By averaging these values, ATR provides a smooth view of recent volatility. 

__How to Read ATR__
- A rising ATR indicates more volatility, meaning larger price swings and greater uncertainty.
- A falling ATR indicates less volatility, meaning smaller price swings.
- The ATR reading itself tells you what move you can expect within the set time period of the ATR (generally 14 days).

__How to Use ATR__
ATR is generally used for 3 things: stop loss selection, profit target selection, and option selling strike selection.

**Stop Loss Selection:**
- Take the ATR reading on the __candle__ you are looking to execute your trade on. Take the ATR reading and multiply it by 1.5. Then subtract it from the candle closing price.
> For example, $AMD on the 30 minute chart is showing an ATR reading of 3.39. 
> So 3.39 * 1.5 = 5.09. 
> Take that result and subtract it from the close of that candle: $233.14 - 5.09 = $228.05. 
> $228.05 is my SL.
- Remember, this is calculated from the last 14 periods. So if using the 30min chart, that means it's from the last 14 candles. Meaning I could expect this target to be hit within the next 14 candles.

**Profit Target Selection:**
- It's basically the same as Stop Loss selection above. Take the ATR reading on the candle and multiply by 1.5. Then add it to the candle closing price.
> Same $AMD example as above. $233.14 + 5.09 = $228.51.
> $228.51 is my PT.
- Remember, this is calculated from the last 14 periods. So if using the 30min chart, that means it's from the last 14 candles. Meaning I could expect this target to be hit within the next 14 candles.

**Strike Selection:**
- I tend to use the Daily Timeframe for this process. It's important to make sure that this isn't used just after an Earnings release or major news event where the stock moves significantly, as that will skew the ATR reading.
- Take the ATR of the latest candle and multiply it by 2. Then add it to the current candle price for Covered Calls or subtract it from the current candle closing price for Cash-Secured Puts.
> For example, looking at $UBER on the Daily Chart on 6/24/25 - the ATR reads 2.87. Let's say I want to sell a CC at a strike where I don't want to get called away.
> 2.87 * 2 = 5.74.
> The current price is $91.65. I'll add 5.74 to get $97.40 dollars.
- I can sell a CC with an expiration date of 7 or 14 DTE at $98 (rounding up).

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/78l/how-to-use-atr-average-true-ra*

### Post #714: 2025-10-31 23:45 — `$AAPL`

**How to use ATR (Average True Range) - Part 3**

This will be the final post in my guide on how to use the ATR indicator. I’ll be showing an option selling example of $AAPL and giving some final thoughts. 

Red on below ⬇️

**$AAPL Cash-Secured Put on Daily Chart**
- MV Bullish Trend Cycle ✅ 
- Price above AVWAP-E ✅ 

Price is in bullish technical structure from the above checklist. I want to go long AAPL, but I'm not sure if it will pullback or not. I'll sell a CSP so I make profit non-directionally.

- ATR = 4.68
- ATR * 2 = 9.36
- Strike = Current Price - 9.36 = ~$230
- Expiration within 14 days = 9/12/25

Price ended up closing above my strike on 9/12 and my CSP expired for 100% profit. If I had gotten assigned, I wouldn't mind since AAPL continued to stay in a bullish structure and would've made money from the price moving higher.

__Final Notes:__
- This isn't the only way to use ATR, but I've generally found success with this process so far. 
- Experiment with ATR on your strategies and see what kind of targets you would've set and what would've resulted.
- A lot of indicators are based around the measure of ATR (Supertrend, Trailing Stop Loss, ADX, etc). This is a good one to have in your toolbelt!

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/8tD/how-to-use-atr-average-true-ra*

- Annualized Quarterly GDP is reduced by 0.2% for each week the shutdown extends 
- A month long shutdown would lead to 43k additional unemployed
- A shutdown leads to pauses and delays to economic data that the Federal Reserve needs to make interest rate decisions

### Post #758: 2025-11-26 19:49 — `$SPY`

**How to Use AVWAP-Q on $SPY | TA Series Part 1**

To be a consistently successful trader or investor, you have to understand how to analyze the market as a whole. AVWAP-Q is one of the most important tools I use for determining short-term market bias, and it has kept me on the right side of major moves time and time again.

In this article, I’ll explain what AVWAP-Q is, how I use it, and show historical examples of how it improves decision-making on $SPY.

__What is AVWAP-Q__
AVWAP-Q (Anchored Volume Weighted Average Price – Quarterly) calculates the average price traders have paid since the beginning of each quarter, weighted by volume.

Quarter anchors begin on:
- January 1
- April 1
- July 1
- October 1

This indicator should only be used on the daily chart and Should only be used on the major indices, specifically $SPY. AVWAP-Q helps you see where the “true cost basis” of the market lies quarter-to-date, which gives clear insight into short-term trend direction.

__How to use AVWAP-Q__
The rules are simple:
- If price is trading above AVWAP-Q, then buyers are in control and the market is in a bullish short-term structure.
- If price is trading below AVWAP-Q, then sellers are in control and the market is in a bearish short-term structure.

It's important to note that I said __the market__ is in a bullish/bearish short-term structure. __That's because most stocks will follow what $SPY does__. That's why I post daily market analysis showcasing several indicators, including AVWAP-Q.

It's also important to wait for __the daily candle to close__ when we get a break of AVWAP-Q to confirm the short-term structure. As you'll see in the examples below, sometimes you will get candle wicks above/below AVWAP-Q that would fake you out if you don't wait for that confirmation.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/bbp5/how-to-use-avwap-q-on-dollarsp*

### Post #762: 2025-11-29 19:36 — `$SPY`

**How to Use AVWAP-Q on $SPY | TA Series Part 2**

For the rest of this series, I’ll be going over examples of how AVWAP-Q can be used to navigate different market conditions and always stay on the right side of the market. 

__Navigating the pullback of July 2024__
AVWAP-Q is the white line on the chart below and is anchored to July 1, 2024. 

The July pullback — caused by the infamous “Yen Carry Trade” unwind — had sharp bounces that tempted traders to call a bottom. AVWAP-Q helped filter those fakeouts, and using this tool, we can breakdown this pullback and see how we would've navigated it successfully:

1/ $SPY went from a bullish to bearish short-term structure on July 18, 2024 with daily candle close AVWAP-Q. At this point, you would de-risk by selling covered calls, reducing position size, and cutting loss on trades that weren't working out. If you're less risk averse, you can hedge using VIX calls, SQQQ shares, or SPY puts.

2/ Buyers tried breaking above AVWAP-Q as seen by the candle wicks above AVWAP-Q - but that's where the importance of waiting for candle close confirmation comes in. Seeing that $SPY continued to close below AVWAP-Q would've kept you bearish in the short-term meaning that you would not have taken long trades, you would've taken the opportunity to de-risk, and you might've even started a short position.

3/ As you can see, price continues to move lower and eventually forms a bottom. So the next question is, when do I go long? You would go long when $SPY __closes__ above AVWAP-Q. And that happens on August 14, 2024. That's the time to sell aggressive CSP's, and OTM Calls at profit targets on your holdings. You can even go long shares or calls on strong stocks or even $SPY with a stop loss at the close of AVWAP-Q.

4/ Like all indicators, AVWAP-Q isn't perfect. As seen above, $SPY closes below AVWAP-Q again on Sept 6, 2024 signaling more potential downside. Again, you would de-risk - but after seeing $SPY close back above AVWAP-Q on Sept 10, 2024, you would do the same game plan you had from August 14, 2024. This final breakout resulted in a 6% move higher in the next month.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/bbyF/how-to-use-avwap-q-on-dollarsp*

### Post #764: 2025-11-30 20:00 — `$SPY`

**How to use AVWAP-Q on $SPY | TA Series Part 3 - Another Example**

__Navigating the rally of late 2017__
AVWAP-Q is the white line on the chart below and is anchored to October 2, 2017.

Sometimes, navigating rallies in the market can be more difficult than pullbacks. You might feel like the rug will be pulled out from under you at any moment. Using AVWAP-Q - you can maintain confidence to hold your positions and even ADD to them as the market continues moving higher. Let's break it down:

1/ $SPY starts in a bullish short-term structure with price continuously trading above AVWAP-Q as the rally continues. This means that you are continuing to trade on the long side as aggressive as your personal risk tolerance allows. 

2/ On October 25, 2017, $SPY sells off sharply down to AVWAP-Q and price broke it intraday (as seen by the daily candle wick). However, you wait for the candle close confirmation and see that price held above AVWAP-Q. This means that you stay bullish expecting price to continue moving higher. Seeing the follow-through on the next day, you can then sell CSPs and even add to your swing positions to profit from the continuation higher.

3/ On November 15, 2017, $SPY again sells off down to AVWAP-Q. Similar to the last pullback, sellers tried breaking it intraday only for buyers to come in and keep it above AVWAP-Q (as seen by the candle wick). This provides another opportunity to sell Cash-Secured Puts and add to swing positions, expecting prices to continue moving higher.

4/ Price then continues to rally strong into the end of the year for a Santa Claus rally. At no point were you de-risking or shorting. You stayed bullish that whole time and took advantage of those small dips to add to longs and sell Cash-Secured Puts.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/bbAh/how-to-use-avwap-q-on-dollarsp*

### Post #767: 2025-12-02 02:35 — `$SPY`

**How to Use AVWAP-Q on $SPY | TA Series Part 4 - Final Example**

If you enjoyed learning technical analysis like this, I have this full series saved as one article in my Discord - which you can access for free in my bio. I have many more guides just like this you can read! 

Here’s the last example for this series ⬇️

__Navigating the Bear Market of 2022__
AVWAP-Q is the white line on the chart below and is anchored to April 1, 2022.

Bear markets are the hardest market conditions to trade successfully. Market conditions are very volatile, with very large and fast moves to both the downside and upside. Again, using AVWAP-Q will help you stay on the right side of the market:

1/ $SPY enters a bearish short-term structure with a close below AVWAP-Q on April 5, 2022. Price continues to grind lower, but you can see buyers still trying to move the market higher. At one point, buyers were able to get $SPY above AVWAP-Q intra-day, only for sellers to step in and close the candle below. After that, the market continued to drop. This would've helped you avoid going long at ANY point after April 5, and maybe even start short positions and getting ready to sell Covered Calls on any positions you're holding for the longer term.

2/ We get a counter-trend rally and price trades right back up to AVWAP-Q. We hang there for a few days only for price to fail to close above it and continue dropping. Seeing this you would've sold more Covered Calls and added to your short positions, expecting price to continue moving lower. 

3/ Using AVWAP-Q, you would've avoided going long at any point during this time period. Instead, you would stay focused on the right side of the market and traded with the prevailing trend - the downside.

### Post #771: 2025-12-03 19:08 — `$SPY`

**How to Use AVWAP-Q on $SPY | TA Series Part 5 - When it Doesn’t Work**

I’m making one additional post in this series that goes over when this indicator doesn’t work, as request by @happypepi 

Read on below ⬇️

__No Indicator is Perfect, and AVWAP-Q is not an Exception__
Luckily there weren't too many example of inconsistencies with this indicator, but I was able to find an example of a period of time where the market was not respecting AVWAP-Q consistently - making it very difficult to trade with conviction. 

This is the period of time before the pullback of 2015. AVWAP-Q is the white line on the chart below and is anchored to April 1, 2015

There were some times without that chop were we did see some direction above or below AVWAP-Q for a few weeks:
- April 6 - April 28
- May 8 - June 15
- June 26 - July 13

However, for the most part $SPY was breaking above and below AVWAP-Q several times in this period until a direction was finally chosen to the downside on August 2015. So what does this mean?

When you start seeing choppy price action around AVWAP-Q like this, this means that you have to treat this as a directionless market environment. You reduce your position sizing on trades you take, you reduce the amount of directional trades, and you focus on selling options. There will be only 2 scenarios that will follow price action like this - the market will either rally or drop. You need to prepare for both scenarios - and the way I do it personally is to prepare my portfolio for a drop.

When you prepare for a drop, you make sure you're only holding profitable companies that you know will recover after the drop, you make sure that you have enough cash on hand to get in when the market drops, and you reduce your position sizing. Instead of selling 2 puts, you sell 1. You sell at more conservative strikes to allow cushion to manage your trade if the market drops. You cut loss on any position that are not doing well - because they'll only do worse on a drop.

This way, when you get the market drop - you are ready to profit from it. If the market rallies instead, then great news - you are also positioned to get in aggressively!

*Link: https://afterhour.com/MarketVictor/bbTJ/how-to-use-avwap-q-on-dollarsp*
